Title: Apologia Peregrinatiey do Kraiow Wschodnych przez mie Meletiusza Smotrzyskieo, Arcybiskupa Polockiego, Episkopa Witebskiego y Mscislawskiego, Archimandryte Wilenskiego y Dermanskiego, Roku P. 1623 y 24 obchodzonej, przez falszywa Bracia slownie y na pismie spotwarzoney, do przezacnego Narodu Ruskiego, obojego karu ludzkie y swietzkie, sporzadzona y podana, A. 1628 (Apologia of my journey to the Eastern lands)
Creator: Meletij Smotryc'kyj (Meletius Smotrzyski), Archbishop of Polotsk, Bishop of Vitebsk and Mstislavl, archimandrite of Vilnius and Derman (as the title page gives him); dedicated to Tomasz Zamoyski; dated at the monastery of Derman, 25 August 1628
Publisher: Drukarnia Jana Szeligi (the colophon reads 'W Drukarniey Jana Szeligi'); place not read
Publication Date: 1628 (title page 'A. 1628, Augusti, Die 25, w Monasteru Dermaniu')
Rights: Public Domain (a 1628 print; the host record states 'Domena publiczna (public domain)')
Language: pol
Source: Internet Archive item jbc.bj.uj.edu.pl.NDIGSTDR013215 (Jagiellonian Digital Library, collection polishpublicdomain), file NDIGSTDR013215_djvu.txt, fetched directly by the library/source-research thread (Era VII session), 2026-10-23; host rights statement: the item record states 'Domena publiczna (public domain)'
Provenance note: Header prepended; the OCR text follows unchanged apart from this header and the long-s normalisation (ſ to s, 0 replaced). Nothing else was corrected, re-spaced or retyped. Rights basis: a print of 1628, centuries older than the 95-year rule, clears the public-domain-by-date rule; the host record states public domain.
Content note: SECOND SOURCE WITNESS FOR QUOTING: the OCR of the 17th-century black-letter print is garbled (about 58,000 tokens, many of them broken), so quotes need the page images (the item carries the PDF). Smotryc'kyj's own defence of his journey to the East in 1623-24 and of his turn to communion with Rome, addressed to the Ruthenian nation and printed in Polish; it argues the procession of the Holy Spirit and the primacy of Rome from the Fathers. The convert's own voice, 1628. OCR sampled at five points (10/30/50/70/90 percent): words recognisable but with many letter errors throughout.
---
